# Test readability ledger

Date: 2026-10-02. Spec: [test readability sweep](2026-10-02-test-readability-design.md).
Status: reviewed and approved by the user on 2026-10-02 (see Review).
The survey used twelve areas, not the spec's ten; the plan (Areas) explains the split.

## Baseline

Measured on 2026-10-02 at commit f9abb76d with `.superpowers/readability/metrics.sh`.

- suites (root): 372
- suites (frontend): 43
- replayed setup files (execute()/initialReady): 7
- Table.start files: 157
- state patches (updateCurrent/copy(current =): 313
- @unchecked: 158
- numeric card ids: 84
- tuple destructures with _: 69
- Table.update escapes: 14
- test files over 800 lines: 4
- largest test file: 1829 src/test/scala/oathdigital/application/GameApplicationServiceSuite.scala

`metrics.sh` counts `frontend/src/test` as well as `src/test`, which is why
@unchecked (158), tuple destructures (69) and test files over 800 lines (4)
exceed the spec's root-only figures (150, 64, 3); numeric card ids (84) count
both trees too. `shared/src/test` is surveyed (area 08) but not counted in the
metrics.

## Review

The user reviewed the ledger on 2026-10-02 and approved it as is, with these
decisions:

1. **00-040 `againstPlayer`.** The helper stays as a helper and is renamed
   `rivalRulesOriginAndHoldsOathkeeper`, so its name states the board it
   builds. This settles the conflict with the spec's example, which named
   `againstPlayer` as a helper that stays.
2. **Rule labels.** The differences between areas (R1 vs R8 for hidden
   defaults, R2 vs R1 for `Table.homeOf`, R2 vs R4 for hand-built decision
   ids) stay as filed. Plan 2 works from each row's planned outcome.
3. **R2 renames on documented helpers** (06-084, 07-058, 07-072, 07-118)
   stay. A helper's name must describe what it does; a doc comment does not
   replace a descriptive name.
4. **Placeholders** such as `<printed name>` in planned outcomes are filled
   in by Plan 2.
5. **The survey prompt's "R1 pass" example** stays as worded. It matters only
   if a survey is rerun.

No rows were struck.

## Calibration

Calibration run: area 08 (model, catalog, serialization; 19 root suites and 6
shared-module suites) surveyed once with the first prompt, then every row
checked against the code on 2026-10-02.

**Area 08 rows.** 48 rows checked. 35 right, 6 right in substance but filed
under the wrong rule, 7 wrong. The wrong-row rate is 13 of 48 (27%), over the
"more than a quarter" threshold. Area 08 is therefore rerun with the tightened
prompt (by the controller, into `rows/08-rerun.md`) to measure the prompt; the
corrected rows below stay as the ledger's area 08. (An earlier pass counted 12 of 48 and kept three marginal rows,
08-013, 08-033 and 08-038, as right; review moved 08-039 to wrong.)

Wrong rows, removed from the rows file (their IDs are left as gaps):

| Row | Filed as | Why it is wrong | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| 08-001 | R8 | The title and the named faces give the 12 and the 0 ((1 + 2) shields doubled twice, a Blank doubled). | R8 on an expectation the body explains |
| 08-007 | R1 | Not a board fact: the private vals sit directly above the test that asserts their wire form. | R1 outside its definition |
| 08-009 | R7 | The title names both assertions in the abstract (identities, required slots); no assertion falls outside it. | R7 on an accurate title |
| 08-016 | R1 | "The rest of the sample game is valid" is the premise of every validate test, not a board fact. | R1 outside its definition |
| 08-021 | R8 | The titles state 6 and 7; the argument order of refresh is not a rubric finding. | R8 on an expectation the title explains |
| 08-022 | R7 | The empty-dice result is still a Conquest, so "both kinds" is accurate. | R7 on an accurate title |
| 08-039 | R8 | "6 cards" is an input label in a round-trip test; no asserted expectation depends on it. | R8 on an input, not an expectation |

Right in substance, wrong rule, corrected in the rows file:

- 08-004, 08-011, 08-015, 08-017: Keep rows filed as R3. The prompt's own
  example says Rule Keep. Now Keep.
- 08-029, 08-044: repeated mechanics (stream boilerplate, an encode-decode
  round trip written 17 times) filed as R2. R2 is for an opaque helper, not
  for missing one. Now R4.

Count corrections in right rows: 08-006 (4 uses of `toOption.get`, not 5),
08-023 (3 uses at lines 32, 33 and 43, not 4), 08-024 (41 tests, not 38),
08-030 (64 uses, not 63), 08-029 (17 stream sites and 14 repeated steps).
Two cross-references fixed: 08-029 cited 08-035 for PayCostWireSuite (it is
08-034); 08-027 and 08-028 now use the names 00-141 and 00-142 propose
(`SetupResult(state, events)`, `setupThroughWake`). 08-020 duplicates 00-150
(TestGameFixtures); it is now R1 and says "count once", so the summary must
not count it twice.

Rows after correction: 41. Per rule: R7 14, R4 8, R1 6, R8 4, Keep 4, R2 1,
R3 1, R6 1, R10 1, R11 1, R5 0.

**Rerun.** Area 08 was rerun with the tightened prompt (`rows/08-rerun.md`)
and each rerun row checked against the code. 42 rows: 40 right, 0 wrong rule,
2 wrong. Wrong-row rate 2 of 42 (4.8%), against 13 of 48 (27%) for the first
run. The two wrong rows: 08-001 (R8 on ExecutableCatalogSuite's `suitOf`: the
printed suits are the oracle of a catalog suite, not an unexplained
expectation) and 08-028 (R1 on `indexWhere(...)` returning -1: a diagnostics
concern, the body already names what it picks). Three right rows are
marginal (08-030 R5 on arbitrary ids in a wire suite, 08-031 a Boolean
assertion helper, 08-037 R2 on a positional fixture that the corrected set
files as R8); counting them wrong gives 5 of 42 (12%). Corrected rows the
rerun missed: 3 outright (08-033, 08-034, 08-048) and part of 3 more (08-013
folded into the rerun's OpaqueIdSuite rename, 08-026's wrong-format-version
assertion, 08-028's wildcard import and `completedValue`). The rerun found
five real findings the corrected set lacked; they were added as 08-049 to
08-053 (ExecutableCatalogSuite `.get` casts, the Visions Drawn replay title,
the synthetic Travel park in the answers test, three numeric ids in the power
note tests, the Boolean `refused` helper), and two existing rows were
extended (08-006 gains PlayerSetupStateSuite:48, 08-025 gains the seventh
version-labelled title at line 703). The corrected set is now 46 rows (R7 16,
R4 10, R1 6, R8 4, Keep 4, R5 1, R2 1, R3 1, R6 1, R10 1, R11 1). Of the
rerun's 16 R7 rows, 10 are clear (a title that is false or misleading, or an
unrelated second subject) and 6 are small title extensions (08-008, 08-015,
08-016, 08-019, 08-024, 08-025), so R7 is not mostly marginal and no R7
example was added. In the rerun, Keep rows are labelled Keep and repeated
mechanics are filed as R4; none of the first run's R8-on-a-title or
R1-on-a-premise misjudgments came back.

**Missing findings.** The seven files with no rows (CatalogHandlerInventorySuite,
ExecutableCatalogSuite, ActionKindSuite, ChronicleSuite, EnumWireKeySuite,
WorldModelSuite, VisionCardPresentationSuite) were all opened; each deserves
none. One borderline case was not added: WorldModelSuite line 28 titles
itself "support Campaign target derivation" while the body only asserts
`sameRuler` and `enemies`. The numeric ids in ExecutableCatalogSuite and
GameEventWireSuite fall under the wire and catalog exemption. No clear
misses.

**Examples added to the prompt** (one per kind of misjudgment; the plan's
Agent prompt and `.superpowers/readability/survey-prompt.md` agree):

- Keep, not R3 (the four mislabeled Keep rows).
- R4 (repeated mechanics, DRY applies), not R2.
- R6: `val (state, events) = execute()` (a tuple fixture the agent found but
  had no example for).
- R7 pass, and when R7 applies: an accurate title over named faces; the
  `usePower` title with an extra field in the body (08-001, 08-009, 08-022).
- R8 pass: `ExileSupply.refresh(9, 1)` under titles that give 6 and 7
  (08-021). No example yet for 08-039 (R8 on an input label); the rerun
  will show whether the prompt needs one.
- R1 pass: "the rest of the game is valid" is not a board fact (08-007,
  08-016).
- One reporting line: every count comes from grep or a line count (four rows
  had a wrong count).

   EndWakeProcedureSuite); row 00-138 puts the fixture's `catalog` in 193 files (agent's count, not

R5 9, R6 9, R9 9, R10 7, R8 3; the spot check re-filed 00-127 and 00-132 from
R3 to Keep). Five rows spot-checked against the code with
`grep -rlw`; caller counts for generic names (`base`, `board`, `staged`,

| Row | Check | Result |
|---|---|---|

| 00-033 CampaignFixture.board | 49 callers; defaults differ from the quiet table | Count right (46 unqualified plus 3 qualified call sites). Corrected the Problem: only `warbands = 5` differs from the quiet 3; `supply = 7` equals the quiet 7. R1 holds. |

One verdict conflicts with the spec text: 00-040 files `againstPlayer` under
R2, while the spec's "Shared fixtures" paragraph names `againstPlayer` as a
helper that stays. The code supports the row (the helper sets 2 warbands at
the origin and hands the Oathkeeper title to the other player, so it hides

or the row overturned before Plan 2 acts on it.

   for the four suites that patch the track; re-filed R3.
4. 08-020 duplicates 00-150 (TestGameFixtures): kept as a row, Planned outcome

**Cross-area notes.** The same cause is sometimes filed under different rules
in different areas. Hidden `staged` defaults behind expected values are R1 in

07-058, 07-072, 07-118); the user's review can strike them.

that stays; the code hides two warbands and the Oathkeeper title. Raised for

## Test doubles and contracts

Plan 2 task 3 gives each fake a contract suite that the fake and the real
implementation both pass (spec, "Test doubles"). One line per double. A stub
(fixed answers) or a spy (records calls) holds no behavior that can drift from
the real class, so it needs no contract.

- `InMemoryEventStreamRepository` (src/main/scala/oathdigital/application/EventStreamRepository.scala:48)
  is a fake of `EventStreamRepository`; the real implementation is
  `HsqldbEventStreamRepository` (src/main/scala/oathdigital/persistence/HsqldbEventStreamRepository.scala:40).
  Needs an abstract `EventStreamRepositoryContract` with a `make()` factory run
  against both, the shared behavior tests moved out of
  `HsqldbEventStreamRepositorySuite`; this closes the ROADMAP item "Contract
  suite for `EventStreamRepository`". No row classifies the class (it is
  production code). The doubles around it need no contract: 01-057 (spy with a
  canned load), 09-028 (`CountingEventStreamRepository`, a spy over this fake),
  09-055 and 09-080 (stubs that fail every call).
- `InMemoryEventJournal` (src/main/scala/oathdigital/engine/Engine.scala:28)
  implements `EventJournal`; `git grep` finds no other `EventJournal`
  implementation (the Hsqldb store implements `EventStreamRepository`, and
  `EventJournalSchema` is only the table) and no caller in `src`, `frontend` or
  `shared`. No real implementation, so no contract; no row classifies it.
- `Totals` in EventReplayEngineSuite (01-127) is a fake of `EventEvolution`;
  the real implementation is `OathRules` (src/main/scala/oathdigital/gameplay/OathRules.scala:40).
  Needs an `EventEvolutionContract` (the initial state is stable; `evolve` is
  pure and returns a Left, not a state, for a corrupt event) run against both.
- `FakeStore` in GameLogOverlaySuite and LogMarkerSuite (11-031, 11-041) is a
  fake of `LogStore`; the real implementation is `LogStore.browser`
  (frontend/src/main/scala/oathdigital/frontend/LogMarker.scala:19, over
  `localStorage`). Needs a `LogStoreContract` (a written key reads back; an
  unknown key reads None) in the frontend tests, run against the one shared
  `FakeLogStore`; the trait's doc says the jsdom harness makes the real store
  throw, so the contract needs a harness with a working `localStorage`.
- `FakePollClock` and `ManualClock` (12-042, 12-044) are fakes of `PollClock`;
  the real implementation is `BrowserPollClock`
  (frontend/src/main/scala/oathdigital/frontend/SnapshotPollingCoordinator.scala:11).
  Needs a `PollClockContract` (a scheduled callback fires once, a cancelled one
  never fires) in the frontend tests, run against the one shared fake and
  against `BrowserPollClock`.
- `FakeClient` in TableSessionSuite (12-044) is a spy that records calls and a
  controllable stub (the test completes each promise); the real
  implementations are `HttpGameClient` and `TrustedHttpGameClient`
  (frontend/src/main/scala/oathdigital/frontend/GameClient.scala:123 and 192).
  It computes no answer of its own, so no contract; Plan 2 documents it as a
  spy, not a fake. `RecordingNavigation` in the same row is a spy.
- `IdentityRepositoryStub`, `failing` and the anonymous resolveSession subclass
  (01-078, 09-072) are stubs (every call fails, or returns a canned
  `StorageFailure`) and a spy (records the thread); the real implementation is
  `HsqldbIdentityRepository`. No fake, so no contract.
- The `CampaignDicePort` doubles (00-014, 01-029, 01-104, 01-126) are stubs
  (fixed faces) and spies (counts rolls); the real implementation is
  `CampaignDicePort.random` (GameRandomPorts.scala:32). No fake, so no
  contract; they share one testkit `Dice` object (00-014).

- `encoded`, `decoded` (wire results) (4 rows): 02-044, 08-023, 08-030, 08-034
- `assertWireRoundTrips`, `assertRoundTrips`, `roundTripStream` (17 rows): 00-090, 04-196, 06-034, 06-039, 06-074, 06-081, 06-088, 06-093, 06-127, 06-133, 06-140, 06-147, 06-153, 06-161, 06-169, 08-029, 08-044
- `authorized`, `created`, `begun`, `parsed`, `updated`, `right` (one row each): 01-080, 09-005, 09-060, 09-069, 11-014, 11-037
- Look reads `discardAfter`, `forceKind`, `boardWarbands` (8 rows): 00-162, 04-009, 04-058, 04-084, 04-166, 05-099, 05-104, 05-143

**Overlaps.**

- `accepted` and `rightOf` both unwrap an Either and fail with the actual
  value. So do `built`, `started`, `opened`, `executed`, `replayed`,
  `authorized`, `created`, `begun`, `parsed`, `updated` and `right` on the Right
  side, and `rejected`, `leftOf`, `assertLeft`, `refusal`, `refused` and
  `failureOf` on the Left side. Rows in different areas name the same
  accessor differently; the Foundation task should settle one generic
  unwrap and keep the domain names only where the type differs.
- `ready(state)` overlaps `Table.ready` (testkit/Table.scala:287) and
  `Situation.ready` (testkit/Situation.scala:55), which turn a Table or
  Situation into its `ReadyGame`, not an `OathState`. `parked` and `finished` overlap
  WhenPlayedHarness (00-136, 00-146). `chooseOne`, `chooseMany` and
  `chooseAmount` all unwrap a parked query.
- The wire accessors (`encoded`, `decoded`, `refusal`, the round-trip
  helpers; rows in area 08) must live in `shared/src/test` where
  the shared protocol suites compile for Scala.js (Calibration, note 1).
  The frontend accessors (11-014, 11-037, 12-049, 12-029) live in
  `frontend/src/test`; no frontend outcome names the root `Table`.

**Settled (Plan 2, Task 1).** One accessor per shape:
`Unwrap.accepted` (a Right: also what rows call `rightOf`, `built`,
`started`, `opened`, `executed`, `replayed`, `authorized`, `created`,
`begun`, `parsed`, `right`, `encoded`, `decoded`), `Unwrap.rejected` (a Left:
`leftOf`, `assertLeft`, `refusal`, `refused`, `failureOf`), `Unwrap.present`
(an Option: `loaded`), `Unwrap.as[T]` (a case: `chooseOne`, `chooseMany`,
`chooseAmount`, `updated`), and in the root testkit `Outcomes.ready`,
`Outcomes.parked`, `Outcomes.finished`, `Outcomes.assertWireRoundTrips`,
`Look.discardAfter` and `Look.forceKind`.

**New Table steps proposed by rows.** Each is a fact that three or more
suites patch by hand (R3), or one the row says a step must reach.

- `relicDeckEmptied`: 04-065, 04-076, 04-172 (3 suites).
- `parkedWalker(action, path = "0")`: 01-099, 01-113 (3 suites).
- `visionsDrawn(n)`: 10-121, 10-129 (4 suites patch the track).
- `revealedVision(player, vision)`: 10-129, 10-167, 10-169 (optional there).
- `backInPhase(phase)`, keeping the turn's used powers: 06-167, 06-181, 06-184 (3 suites).
- A `face` argument on the existing `peoplesFavor` and `darkestSecret`:
  00-097 (the origin), 01-001, 03-109, 06-014, 06-023, 06-030, 06-052, 10-077.

Rows that ask only for an existing step (`.secrets`, `.tokens`, `.pawn`,
`.siteTokens`, `.turn`) are not listed. Rows that keep a patch with a
"no Table step ..." comment are not requests for a step.

## Mutations

Each fix task breaks three to five production rules its rewritten tests
guard, one at a time, and records the result here.

| Task | Rule broken (file:line: change) | Test that failed | Result |
|---|---|---|---|

## 08-model-catalog-serialization

| ID | File | Test | Rule | Problem | Planned outcome | Outcome | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 08-002 | oathdigital/model/ActionValuesSuite.scala:16 | procedure references form three families with keys unique across all | R7 | Title names the three families and key uniqueness; the body also asserts fromKey and fromFamilyKey routing for end-wake, the triggered family being not startable, and the full TriggeredProcedureRef list. The suite name (ActionValues) also covers refs and dice. | Split into: "ref keys are listed per family and unique across them", "a key resolves only under its own family" (end-wake, recover), and "a triggered procedure cannot be started by key alone" (oathkeeper, forced-wake). Rename the file to say dice scoring and procedure refs, or move the ref tests to ProcedureRefSuite. | | |
| 08-003 | oathdigital/model/CardIndexSuite.scala:6 | container membership derives a card's location | R1 | Expected locations depend on TestGameFixtures placements the body never states: worldDenizen is the world deck's only card (index 0), siteDenizen is the first site's only denizen, storedEdifice is in the Atlas stored site "S9" at position 0. | Add a comment per assertion naming where the fixture puts the card (world deck top, first site, Atlas stored site S9), or build the three placements in the test body. | | |
| 08-004 | oathdigital/model/CardIndexSuite.scala:38 | the derived index rejects a card present in two containers; temporary hands and set-aside relics are indexed as owning zones; temporary hands remain indexed even when their owner is invalid | Keep | Three tests patch the fixture with game.copy chains three levels deep (map.sites.updated, commonCards.copy, temporaryHands). The patched field is the subject, and Table steps keep the card inventory whole so they cannot make a duplicate. | Keep. Add a comment to each: "copy, not a Table step: the test makes an illegal state on purpose". | | |
